data-meta="0_96"><div class="phabricator-remarkup"><p>What do you think about putting this change behind a pref?</p>

<p>The pref can be enabled by default, but e.g. it would allow further investigation of the site by the performance team by disabling the pref, and also in case this patch causes any performance regressions (for example, if a site removes an apz-aware listener but we don&#039;t clear the fast-path entry so we continue waiting for a content response), having the pref makes it easier to confirm this patch as the cause.</p>

<p>(The only codepath that would need to be guarded by the pref is the call to  <tt class="remarkup-monospaced">BrowserChild::NotifyApzAwareListenerAdded()</tt> in <tt class="remarkup-monospaced">EventStateManager.cpp</tt>. The rest of the machinery can remain as-is, since it will essentially be a no-op if no one has called <tt class="remarkup-monospaced">NotifyApzAwareListenerAdded</tt>.)</p></div></span><table class="phabricator-inline-summary-table"><tr><th colspan="3" class="inline-comment-summary-table-header"><span class="visual-only phui-icon-view phui-font-fa fa-file-code-o darkbluetext mmr" data-meta="0_259" aria-hidden="true"></span>dom/events/EventListenerManager.cpp</th></tr>

<tr><td class="inline-line-number inline-table-dolumn"><a href="#inline-1634639" class="num"><span class="visual-only phui-icon-view phui-font-fa fa-share darkbluetext mmr" data-meta="0_261" aria-hidden="true"></span>235</a></td><td class="inline-summary-content inline-table-dolumn" colspan="2"><div class="phabricator-remarkup"><span class="transaction-comment" data-sigil="transaction-comment" data-meta="0_98"><div class="phabricator-remarkup"><p>Can log modules be shared by different source files in this way?</p>

<p>(If so, we can simplify some existing code which shares them in a more laborious way. For example, APZCTreeManager declares one in <a href="https://searchfox.org/firefox-main/rev/50abc35bf42ac7aede2b0a18f3cd10c266cc41d5/gfx/layers/apz/src/APZCTreeManager.h#137" class="remarkup-link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">the header</a>, so that <a href="https://searchfox.org/firefox-main/rev/50abc35bf42ac7aede2b0a18f3cd10c266cc41d5/gfx/layers/apz/src/WRHitTester.cpp#17" class="remarkup-link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">WRHitTester</a> can share it; I guess it doesn&#039;t need to do that?)</p></div></span></div></td></tr>

<div class="remarkup-reply-head">In <a href="/D301665#10492625" class="phui-tag-view phui-tag-type-object " data-sigil="hovercard" data-meta="0_23"><span class="phui-tag-core phui-tag-color-object">D301665#10492625</span></a>, <a href="/p/botond/" class="phui-tag-view phui-tag-type-person " data-sigil="hovercard" data-meta="0_24"><span class="phui-tag-core phui-tag-color-person">@botond</span></a> wrote:</div>
<div class="remarkup-reply-body"><p>What do you think about putting this change behind a pref?</p>

<p>The pref can be enabled by default, but e.g. it would allow further investigation of the site by the performance team by disabling the pref, and also in case this patch causes any performance regressions (for example, if a site removes an apz-aware listener but we don&#039;t clear the fast-path entry so we continue waiting for a content response), having the pref makes it easier to confirm this patch as the cause.</p>

<p>(The only codepath that would need to be guarded by the pref is the call to  <tt class="remarkup-monospaced">BrowserChild::NotifyApzAwareListenerAdded()</tt> in <tt class="remarkup-monospaced">EventStateManager.cpp</tt>. The rest of the machinery can remain as-is, since it will essentially be a no-op if no one has called <tt class="remarkup-monospaced">NotifyApzAwareListenerAdded</tt>.)</p></div>
</blockquote>

<p>That&#039;s a great idea. I&#039;ve added a new pref named <tt class="remarkup-monospaced">apz.fastpath_apz_aware_listener.enabled</tt>. Though I&#039;ve enclosed more part in the pref since outputting the pref relevant stuff via MOZ_LOG sounds odd.</p></div></span><table class="phabricator-inline-summary-table"><tr><th colspan="3" class="inline-comment-summary-table-header"><span class="visual-only phui-icon-view phui-font-fa fa-file-code-o darkbluetext mmr" data-meta="0_290" aria-hidden="true"></span>dom/events/EventListenerManager.cpp</th></tr>

<tr><td class="inline-line-number inline-table-dolumn"><a href="#inline-1634803" class="num"><span class="visual-only phui-icon-view phui-font-fa fa-share darkbluetext mmr" data-meta="0_309" aria-hidden="true"></span>644</a></td><td class="inline-summary-content inline-table-dolumn" colspan="2"><div class="phabricator-remarkup"><span class="transaction-comment" data-sigil="transaction-comment" data-meta="0_121"><div class="phabricator-remarkup"><p>My intention is that it can be used to narrow down the precise event target. For example, given that there&#039;s an overflow: hidden scroll container having an element which will be an event target and a cross-origin iframe, the event target is occupied the top half of the container area, and the iframe occupied the bottom half of the area. So the <tt class="remarkup-monospaced">overflow: hidden</tt> container is <strong>layerized</strong> in terms our old architecture.  Then if we send the overflow:hidden scroll container&#039;s id to APZ, then it will be close to the correct element, right?</p>

<p>Without sending the overflow: hidden scroll Id, say, the root container is the only one scroll container other than it, then events outside of the overflow:hidden container also needs to wait for a content response.</p>

<p>I do even expect, in future once after we are sure this fast-patch can be applicable for other cases, then if each event target element had an id, which will be notified to WebRender, then we can use the element id for an additional hit-testing without flushing WebRender display items, even if the items are stale, it&#039;s more accurate than just using scroll ids.</p></div></span></div></td></tr>

involving paint for other cases, say, for example given that a site sets up a wheel event listener in response to mouse click, but in the mouse click event handler there&#039;s anything else, then we don&#039;t need to paint, right?</p></div></span></div></td></tr></table></div></div></div></div></div></div><div class="phui-timeline-event-view phui-timeline-spacer"></div><div
